## waypoints

1. **W1989** (working) The hooks mutants shards take the module they plant in and the tests whose closure holds it as
   their inputs
   - next: Run the whole commit_kata site suite (and the other hooks sites) green, then retire the sharded hooks
     :mutants in favour of the per-site targets; the survivors that remain, if any, are attributed the same way. I still
     need the full site run, W1989
2. **W1898** (working) The watchdog and tick-open resolve their tools from the writer's own installation, and
   tick-open's timing is fixed
   - next: Find where --tick-open runs the tick hooks (pathsforward tickhooks.py and the cli's --tick-open) and wrap
     each in a resource.getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN) / perf_counter pair; print one 'slowest: `<hook>` wall Ns cpu Ns'
     line; emit mikemol.tick.hook_seconds{hook,kind=wall|cpu} through mikemol-otlp. After the tool-resolution half of
     this card (the PATH lookups). I still need to read how --tick-open runs the hooks, W1898
3. **W2013** (working) importdag emits one input-mutant target per test and removable edge, wrapped to pass only on
   failure
   - next: Generator: one target per pair from graph.input_mutants, an inner py_test with the edge removed wrapped by an
     inverting sh_test; shape of check_mutants/mutants_gen.py; then the survivor report naming reading (1) over-declared
     and (2) an untested branch.
4. **W2079** (working) A retro scaffold builder turns an event record into a --add-retro file pre-filled with G0 (the
   card's next step at submit) and G1 (the frozen gate record)
   - next: Define the input first: the event record is the verdict row W2128 ships (event verdict, repo, verdict,
     reason, rc, waypoints, stack_key; landed 56a13d3, live once the hooks venv is rebuilt). New pure function
     pathsforward/src/mikemol/pathsforward/retroscaffold.py: scaffold(event, queue) -> Json building a retro dict for
     --add-retro with G0 = the next_bounded_step of each card in event['waypoints'] as it stood in the queue (read from
     the HEAD copy, not the working one), G1 = the frozen record (verdict, reason, rc, key), findings empty and
     enforcement_layer unset, so retros.retro_refusals accepts it as a scaffold and the reviewer fills the findings.
     Tests: a REFUSED row with two waypoints yields G0 for both and G1 with the reason; a row naming a waypoint absent

147. **W841** (blocked) bazel admits each ACTION before it materializes data into zram, letting running actions finish
   and zram's LRU GC drain between them
   - blocked on: W1667
   - next: Operator 2026-10-06 (replaces the watchdog/stop-a-running-job design): the key is an admission check prior to
     the executor copying data into zram, catching actions as they are about to begin; existing actions continue; zram's
     own GC cycle drains it LRU-style, so waiting for it to fall is sound (not a permanent pin). Measured on this bazel
     (bazel help build --long): no built-in dynamic per-action gate, but three native pieces. (1)
     --local_extra_resources=zram_mb=N plus target tag resources:zram_mb:X makes bazel's own scheduler queue an action
     until the resource frees (static N per command: size it from zram headroom at command start minus a margin; only
     tagged targets take part, so tag them in dist_checks/gate_stages). (2) --run_under (tests) and --shell_executable
     (shell actions) run a wrapper before each such action: wait there on the LIVE zram reading, with membudget's zram
     gate (W837) as the waiter. (3) --sandbox_base picks where per-action sandbox trees go. Not yet shown: a hook that
     gates every local action type dynamically (compile/py actions run through the sandbox binary, not a shell). Next:
     prototype (2) for tests and (1) for the heavy macro targets on mtools, measure whether the heavy zram writers are
     covered, and only then decide whether the remainder needs remote execution (remote_executor is already configured).
     Still open from the earlier measurement: the staged-tree wrapper path and adoption in the other bazel repos.
